     1→# Gap Closure Plan: From Grounded Entities to Join-Ready Structured Data
     2→
     3→## Executive Summary
     4→
     5→The pipeline currently extracts criteria, decomposes entities, grounds them to terminology codes (UMLS CUI, SNOMED, RxNorm, etc.), and generates Entity-Relation-Value-Unit field mappings. Two fundamental problems prevent the output from being join-ready:
     6→
     7→1. **The field mappings are disconnected from the grounded codes.** `field_mapper.py` outputs `{entity, relation, value, unit}` as bare strings with no `entity_concept_id`, and `persist.py` overwrites (rather than accumulates) mappings per criterion via fragile substring match. No single record ties a concept code to a relational operator and value.
     8→
     9→2. **Single-path grounding is insufficient for OMOP interoperability.** The current MedGemma/ToolUniverse path produces UMLS CUIs, SNOMED codes, etc., but not OMOP standard `concept_id`s — the identifiers CIRCE, Atlas, and any OMOP CDM cohort evaluation expect. A parallel OMOP vocabulary mapping path is needed.
    10→
    11→This document identifies each gap precisely, proposes a phased closure plan (aligned with the project's master plan), and references the state of the art.
    12→
    13→---
    14→
    15→## 1. Current State
    16→
    17→| Component | Status | Key files |
    18→|-----------|--------|-----------|
    19→| **Extraction** | Working. Produces `text`, `criteria_type`, `category`, `temporal_constraint`, `numeric_thresholds`, `assertion_status`, `confidence`. | `nodes/extract.py`, `schemas/extraction.py` |
    20→| **Parse** | Working. Decomposes criterion into typed entity mentions (Condition, Medication, Lab_Value, Procedure, Demographic). | `nodes/parse.py`, `prompts/entity_decompose.jinja2` |
    21→| **Ground** | Working. Routes through UMLS/SNOMED/RxNorm/ICD-10/LOINC/HPO APIs, MedGemma selects best candidate, agentic retry on failure. 88% success rate, ~0.88 avg confidence. | `nodes/ground.py`, `schemas/grounding.py`, `config/routing.yaml` |
    22→| **Field Mapping** | Working but **unlinked**. Gemini generates `{entity, relation, value, unit}` — no concept IDs. | `tools/field_mapper.py` |
    23→| **Persist** | Working but **lossy**. Entity records get the code. Field mappings go to `Criteria.conditions["field_mappings"]` but are **overwritten** per entity, and carry no concept ID. | `nodes/persist.py` |
    24→| **Entity table** | Has `umls_cui`, `snomed_code`, `rxnorm_code`, `icd10_code`, `loinc_code`, `hpo_code`. No `omop_concept_id`. | `libs/shared/src/shared/models.py` |
    25→
    26→### Target: What a join-ready record looks like
    27→
    28→```
    29→atomic_criteria row:
    30→  entity_concept_id  = "C4521885"    ← from MedGemma/ToolUniverse
    31→  entity_concept_system = "umls"     ← from MedGemma/ToolUniverse
    32→  omop_concept_id    = "21604490"    ← from OMOP vocabulary lookup
    33→  relation_operator  = ">="          ← from field mapping
    34→  value_numeric      = 6.5           ← from field mapping
    35→  unit_text          = "%"           ← from field mapping
    36→  unit_concept_id    = 8554          ← from UCUM normalization (OMOP unit)
    37→  criterion_id       = FK
    38→  protocol_id        = FK
    39→  inclusion_exclusion = "inclusion"
    40→```
    41→
    42→This enables: `WHERE patient.measurement_concept_id = 21604490 AND value_as_number >= 6.5`.
    43→
    44→---
    45→
    46→## 2. Gap Analysis
    47→
    48→### Gap 1: Field mappings lack concept IDs
    49→
    50→**Where**: `tools/field_mapper.py` lines 133–141.
    51→
    52→The returned dicts are `{entity, relation, value, unit}` — all strings. The `EntityGroundingResult` passed in *has* `selected_code` and `selected_system`, but these are never propagated into the mapping dicts.
    53→
    54→**Impact**: Field mappings cannot be joined against patient data indexed by concept ID.
    55→
    56→**Fix**: Enrich each mapping dict:
    57→
    58→```python
    59→mappings = [
    60→    {
    61→        "entity": m.entity,
    62→        "relation": m.relation,
    63→        "value": m.value,
    64→        "unit": m.unit,
    65→        "entity_concept_id": entity.selected_code,
    66→        "entity_concept_system": entity.selected_system,
    67→        "omop_concept_id": entity.omop_concept_id,  # from Phase 1a
    68→    }
    69→    for m in response.mappings
    70→]
    71→```
    72→
    73→### Gap 2: Field mappings are overwritten, not accumulated
    74→
    75→**Where**: `persist.py` `_find_criterion_and_update_mappings()` line 143.
    76→
    77→```python
    78→criterion.conditions = {**existing, "field_mappings": field_mappings}
    79→```
    80→
    81→This replaces the `field_mappings` key each time. If a criterion mentions "HbA1c >= 6.5% AND eGFR >= 30 mL/min", whichever entity is persisted last wins.
    82→
    83→**Impact**: Multi-entity criteria lose all but one entity's decomposition.
    84→
    85→**Fix**: Accumulate:
    86→
    87→```python
    88→existing_mappings = existing.get("field_mappings", [])
    89→if isinstance(existing_mappings, list):
    90→    existing_mappings.extend(field_mappings)
    91→else:
    92→    existing_mappings = field_mappings
    93→criterion.conditions = {**existing, "field_mappings": existing_mappings}
    94→```
    95→
    96→### Gap 3: Criterion ↔ Entity matching uses fragile substring search
    97→
    98→**Where**: `persist.py` line 131.
    99→
   100→```python
   101→.where(Criteria.text.contains(entity_text[:50]))
   102→```
   103→
   104→Truncates entity text to 50 chars and does `LIKE '%...%'`. If two criteria contain overlapping text, the wrong criterion gets the mapping. The parse node already associates entities with criterion IDs, but this is not threaded through ground to persist.
   105→
   106→**Impact**: Field mappings can land on wrong criterion records.
   107→
   108→**Fix**: Thread `criterion_id` from parse → ground → persist. Look up Criteria by ID, not substring.
   109→
   110→### Gap 4: No OMOP standard concept_id mapping path
   111→
   112→**Where**: `ground.py` uses only MedGemma + ToolUniverse (UMLS, SNOMED, RxNorm, etc.). These produce source-vocabulary codes, not OMOP standard `concept_id`s.
   113→
   114→OMOP CDM tables (`CONDITION_OCCURRENCE`, `MEASUREMENT`, `DRUG_EXPOSURE`) use OMOP `concept_id` as their primary identifier. CIRCE cohort definitions reference these. Without `omop_concept_id`, export to Atlas/CIRCE requires an additional mapping step at query time, which is error-prone and breaks the "simple join" goal.
   115→
   116→**Impact**: Cannot produce CIRCE-exportable cohort definitions or join directly against OMOP CDM patient data.
   117→
   118→**Fix**: Add a parallel OMOP vocabulary mapping branch (Phase 1a below). Three implementation options exist:
   119→
   120→1. **Athena vocabulary in local DB** — OMOP dictionary files from Athena are located at `data/omop_vocab`. Load these tables (`CONCEPT`, `CONCEPT_SYNONYM`, `CONCEPT_RELATIONSHIP`) into a Postgres instance. For each entity text, run term-similarity search on `concept_name` / `concept_synonym_name` filtered to `standard_concept = 'S'`. This replicates Usagi's automatic matching without requiring the Java GUI. Athena's Solr-based search weights `concept_code` (80K) > `concept_name` (60K) > `concept_synonym_name` (40K).
   121→
   122→2. **OMOPHub REST API** — Third-party API at omophub.com that exposes SNOMED/ICD-10/LOINC/RxNorm concept search over Athena vocabularies with no local DB setup. Good for prototyping; introduces external dependency.
   123→
   124→3. **Athena-Client Python SDK** — Community SDK (Stanford) with `ConceptExplorer` and `ConceptSetGenerator` modules for concept lookup and relationship navigation. Depends on undocumented Athena endpoints.
   125→
   126→**Recommendation**: Option 1 (local Athena DB) for production reliability; Option 2 for rapid prototyping.
   127→
   128→### Gap 5: No expression tree / composite logic structure
   129→
   130→**Where**: Doesn't exist yet.
   131→
   132→The system stores flat field mappings per criterion but has no representation of boolean logic (AND/OR/NOT). Criteria2Query 3.0 and OHDSI CIRCE both use expression trees.
   133→
   134→**Impact**: Cannot correctly evaluate "SpO₂ > 93% AND (respiratory rate < 24 OR on supplemental O₂)" — the OR grouping is lost.
   135→
   136→### Gap 6: No normalized atomic_criteria / composite_criteria tables
   137→
   138→**Where**: Only Entity table and `Criteria.conditions` JSONB exist.
   139→
   140→The target architecture calls for normalized `atomic_criteria`, `composite_criteria`, `criterion_relationships` tables so SQL can join directly on `entity_concept_id` + `relation_operator` + `value_numeric`.
   141→
   142→**Impact**: No surface for patient matching joins.
   143→
   144→### Gap 7: No value/unit normalization
   145→
   146→**Where**: `field_mapper.py` stores units as free-text ("mg/dL", "%", "years").
   147→
   148→EHR data uses UCUM codes (OMOP `unit_concept_id`). The JMIR 2025 study on LLM-based OMOP query generation found unit conversion failures caused 40% of evaluated criteria to fail in practice (hemoglobin Jaccard=0.00).
   149→
   150→**Impact**: Unit mismatches break joins against real EHR data.
   151→
   152→### Gap 8: numeric_thresholds disconnected from entities
   153→
   154→**Where**: `Criteria.numeric_thresholds` has `{value, unit, comparator, upper_value}` but is disconnected from Entity records.
   155→
   156→**Impact**: Concept ID and threshold exist as separate unlinked records. Fixing Gaps 1-3 resolves this — the field mapper already combines entity + threshold; adding the concept ID completes the link.
   157→
   158→---
   159→
   160→## 3. Closure Plan (Phased)
   161→
   162→### Priority Gate: Phase 1a + 1b Must Complete Before Any Phase 2+ Work Begins
   163→
   164→Until concept IDs and relational thresholds are linked atomically in a single record, every downstream structure (expression trees, normalized tables, patient matching, CIRCE export) is built on sand. **No work on Phase 2 or beyond may start until Phase 1b is in QA** — meaning: a protocol has been processed end-to-end, and we can demonstrate that `Criteria.conditions.field_mappings` contains accumulated, concept-ID-bearing records for every grounded entity in the batch.
   165→
   166→This is not just a sequencing preference; it is a hard gate. The reason: if we build expression trees over unlinked field mappings, we create structure around data that cannot be joined, and we'll have to tear it down and rebuild once the foundation is fixed. Fix the atom first, then compose.
   167→
   168→---
   169→
   170→### Phase 1a: Parallel Dual Grounding — MedGemma/ToolUniverse + OMOP Vocabulary (Gap 4)
   171→
   172→**Effort**: ~3-4 days. **Dependency**: None (runs in parallel with existing grounding).
   173→
   174→**Goal**: Run two independent concept-mapping paths in parallel so every entity gets both a source-vocabulary code (UMLS/SNOMED/RxNorm from ToolUniverse) *and* an OMOP standard `concept_id` (from Athena vocabulary). Agreement between the two boosts confidence; disagreements are flagged for HITL.
   175→
   176→**Why dual grounding matters**:
   177→- MedGemma/ToolUniverse gives rich multi-terminology codes and is already in the pipeline.
   178→- OMOP `concept_id` is what CIRCE, Atlas, and OMOP CDM cohort evaluation expect. Without it, every downstream consumer must map from UMLS/SNOMED to OMOP — a lossy, error-prone step.
   179→- When both paths agree (same concept or OMOP concept consistent with SNOMED), we get highest-confidence join-ready criteria. When they disagree, we surface it for review.
   180→
   181→**Implementation**:
   182→
   183→1. **OMOP mapper** (`tools/omop_mapper.py`):
   184→   - **Data location**: OMOP dictionary files from Athena are available at `data/omop_vocab`. Load these tables (`CONCEPT`, `CONCEPT_SYNONYM`, `CONCEPT_RELATIONSHIP`) into the existing Postgres instance (one-time import via `\COPY` or equivalent).
   185→   - For each entity text + entity_type, query: `SELECT concept_id, concept_name, vocabulary_id, standard_concept, domain_id FROM concept WHERE standard_concept = 'S' AND concept_name ILIKE '%{entity_text}%' ORDER BY ...`. Also search `concept_synonym` for synonym matches.
   186→   - Score by term similarity (pg_trgm or Python-side fuzzy match). Return top candidate as `omop_concept_id`.
   187→   - Map entity_type to OMOP domain filter: Condition → `domain_id = 'Condition'`, Medication → `domain_id = 'Drug'`, Lab_Value → `domain_id = 'Measurement'`, Procedure → `domain_id = 'Procedure'`.
   188→
   189→2. **Pipeline integration** (`ground.py`):
   190→   - After parse, fork into two concurrent paths per entity via `asyncio.gather`:
   191→     - Branch A (existing): MedGemma + TerminologyRouter → `entity_concept_id`, `entity_concept_system`.
   192→     - Branch B (new): OMOP mapper → `omop_concept_id`.
   193→   - Reconcile per entity:
   194→     - Both succeed, agree → `reconciliation_status = "agree"`, high confidence.
   195→     - Both succeed, disagree → `reconciliation_status = "disagreement"`, flag for HITL.
   196→     - Only A succeeds → `reconciliation_status = "omop_missing"`.
   197→     - Only B succeeds → `reconciliation_status = "tooluniverse_missing"`.
   198→     - Neither → flag for expert review (existing behavior).
   199→
   200→3. **Schema** (`shared/models.py`):
   201→   - Add `Entity.omop_concept_id: str | None` and `Entity.reconciliation_status: str | None`.
   202→   - Alembic migration to add columns.
   203→
   204→**Validation**: Process a protocol. For each entity, verify `omop_concept_id IS NOT NULL` when OMOP vocabulary contains a match. Check reconciliation_status distribution. Verify OMOP concept IDs are valid standard concepts via Athena lookup.
   205→
   206→---
   207→
   208→### Phase 1b: Wire Concept IDs into Field Mappings (Gaps 1, 2, 3)
   209→
   210→**Effort**: ~1-2 days. **Dependency**: Phase 1a (provides `omop_concept_id`).
   211→
   212→1. **`field_mapper.py`**: Add `entity_concept_id`, `entity_concept_system`, and `omop_concept_id` to each mapping dict from the reconciled `EntityGroundingResult`.
   213→2. **`ground.py`**: Include `criterion_id` in each `EntityGroundingResult` (threaded from parse node's entity-criterion association).
   214→3. **`persist.py`**:
   215→   - Look up Criteria by `criterion_id`, not substring match.
   216→   - Accumulate `field_mappings` (extend list, don't overwrite).
   217→   - Each mapping carries `{entity, relation, value, unit, entity_concept_id, entity_concept_system, omop_concept_id}`.
   218→
   219→**Validation**: Query `Criteria.conditions->'field_mappings'` and verify every mapping has non-null `entity_concept_id`. Verify multi-entity criteria accumulate all mappings. Verify `omop_concept_id` present where OMOP mapping succeeded.
   220→
   221→---
   222→
   223→### Phase 2: Expression Tree + Normalized Tables (Gaps 5, 6)
   224→
   225→**Effort**: ~5-7 days. **Dependency**: Phase 1b must be in QA (see Priority Gate above).
   226→
   227→**Design principle**: The initial implementation must support AND, OR, and NOT from day one — not just AND-only trees with OR/NOT deferred. The reason: if the schema and evaluator only handle conjunctions, we bake in a structural limitation that forces a rewrite when disjunctions are needed. Since ~20% of clinical trial criteria contain OR or NOT logic (e.g., "SpO₂ > 93% AND (respiratory rate < 24 OR on supplemental O₂)"), an AND-only tree silently misrepresents a significant fraction of criteria. Building the tree correctly once is cheaper than building it twice.
   228→
   229→#### 2.1 Schema migration (Alembic)
   230→
   231→- Add `Criteria.structured_criterion` (JSONB).
   232→- Create `atomic_criteria`: `id, criterion_id, protocol_id, inclusion_exclusion, entity_concept_id, entity_concept_system, omop_concept_id, entity_domain, relation_operator, value_numeric, value_text, value_concept_id, unit_text, unit_concept_id, negation, temporal_constraint, original_text, confidence_score, human_verified, human_modified, created_at, updated_at`.
   233→  - Indexes: `(protocol_id, inclusion_exclusion)`, `(entity_concept_id)`, `(omop_concept_id)`, `(criterion_id)`.
   234→- Create `composite_criteria`: `id, criterion_id, protocol_id, inclusion_exclusion, logic_operator ("AND" | "OR" | "NOT"), parent_criterion_id, original_text, human_verified`.
   235→- Create `criterion_relationships`: `parent_criterion_id, child_criterion_id, child_sequence`.
   236→
   237→#### 2.2 Structure builder (new `nodes/structure.py` or `tools/structure_builder.py`)
   238→
   239→Two-pass approach:
   240→
   241→1. **Pass 1 — LLM logic detection**: For each criterion, send the criterion text + its accumulated field_mappings to Gemini with a structured-output prompt that returns the expression tree directly. The prompt receives the atomic field mappings (already concept-grounded from Phase 1b) and asks only for the boolean structure: which atomics are AND'd, which are OR'd, which are negated, and how they nest. This is a much simpler task than entity extraction or grounding — the LLM just classifies the logical connectives. Example prompt output:
   242→   ```json
   243→   {
   244→     "logic_operator": "AND",
   245→     "children": [
   246→       { "field_mapping_index": 0 },
   247→       {
   248→         "logic_operator": "OR",
   249→         "children": [
   250→           { "field_mapping_index": 1 },
   251→           { "field_mapping_index": 2, "negation": true }
   252→         ]
   253→       }
   254→     ]
   255→   }
   256→   ```
   257→
   258→2. **Pass 2 — Fallback**: If the LLM fails or confidence is low, default to AND-of-all-atomics (the safe conservative interpretation). Flag with `structure_confidence = "fallback"` for HITL review.
   259→
   260→After tree construction:
   261→- Parse `value` into `value_numeric` (float) where possible, else `value_text`.
   262→- Write `structured_criterion` JSONB.
   263→- Populate `atomic_criteria` / `composite_criteria` / `criterion_relationships`.
   264→
   265→#### 2.3 Recursive evaluation support
   266→
   267→The composite evaluator must handle all three operators from the start:
   268→
   269→```sql
   270→CASE cc.logic_operator
   271→    WHEN 'AND' THEN BOOL_AND(child.result)
   272→    WHEN 'OR'  THEN BOOL_OR(child.result)
   273→    WHEN 'NOT' THEN NOT BOOL_OR(child.result)
   274→END
   275→```
   276→
   277→This recursive CTE (per `output_criteria.md`) works identically whether the tree is a flat AND or a deeply nested AND/OR/NOT. Building it correctly now means Phase 4 (patient matching) can consume any tree shape without modification.
   278→
   279→#### 2.4 Pipeline integration
   280→
   281→`ingest → extract → parse → ground → persist → structure`.
   282→
   283→**Validation**:
   284→- `SELECT * FROM atomic_criteria WHERE entity_concept_id = 'C0019018' AND relation_operator = '>=' AND value_numeric = 6.5` returns expected rows.
   285→- Every criterion has a `structured_criterion` JSONB.
   286→- For a criterion with OR logic (e.g., "respiratory rate < 24 OR on supplemental O₂"), verify `composite_criteria` contains a row with `logic_operator = 'OR'` and correct children in `criterion_relationships`.
   287→- Recursive CTE evaluation produces correct boolean results for test cases with AND, OR, NOT, and nested combinations.
   288→
   289→---
   290→
   291→### Phase 3: Value and Unit Normalization (Gap 7)
   292→
   293→**Effort**: ~1-2 days. **Dependency**: Phase 2.
   294→
   295→1. **Static UCUM lookup**: `config/ucum_mappings.yaml` mapping free-text units to UCUM codes and OMOP `unit_concept_id`s:
   296→   ```yaml
   297→   "%":      { ucum: "%",     omop_concept_id: 8554 }
   298→   "mg/dL":  { ucum: "mg/dL", omop_concept_id: 8840 }
   299→   "years":  { ucum: "a",     omop_concept_id: 9448 }
   300→   "kg/m2":  { ucum: "kg/m2", omop_concept_id: 9531 }
   301→   "mL/min": { ucum: "mL/min", omop_concept_id: 8795 }
   302→   ```
   303→   ~50 entries covers >95% of clinical trial units.
   304→
   305→2. **In structure builder**: Look up `unit_concept_id` from the UCUM map; store in `atomic_criteria.unit_concept_id`.
   306→
   307→3. **Value normalization**: For categorical values ("positive", "negative"), map to SNOMED qualifier value concepts. Lower priority.
   308→
   309→**Validation**: Spot-check common units resolve correctly.
   310→
   311→---
   312→
   313→### Phase 4: Patient Matching Infrastructure
   314→
   315→**Effort**: ~3-5 days. **Dependency**: Phase 2.
   316→
   317→1. **`patient_criterion_matches` table**: `patient_id, criterion_id, matches (bool), evaluated_at, evaluation_data (JSONB)`. PK `(patient_id, criterion_id)`. Index `(patient_id, criterion_id, matches)`.
   318→2. **Evaluation pipeline**: Batch job reads `atomic_criteria`, evaluates against patient data (OMOP CDM tables via `omop_concept_id`), writes results. Recursive CTE for composite evaluation per `output_criteria.md`.
   319→3. **Trial matching query**:
   320→   ```sql
   321→   SELECT patient_id FROM patients p
   322→   WHERE NOT EXISTS (
   323→     SELECT 1 FROM patient_criterion_matches pcm
   324→     JOIN atomic_criteria ac ON pcm.criterion_id = ac.id
   325→     WHERE ac.protocol_id = ? AND ac.inclusion_exclusion = 'inclusion'
   326→       AND pcm.patient_id = p.patient_id AND pcm.matches = FALSE
   327→   )
   328→   AND NOT EXISTS (
   329→     SELECT 1 FROM patient_criterion_matches pcm
   330→     JOIN atomic_criteria ac ON pcm.criterion_id = ac.id
   331→     WHERE ac.protocol_id = ? AND ac.inclusion_exclusion = 'exclusion'
   332→       AND pcm.patient_id = p.patient_id AND pcm.matches = TRUE
   333→   );
   334→   ```
   335→
   336→**Note**: Requires a defined patient data source (OMOP CDM or similar).
   337→
   338→---
   339→
   340→### Phase 5: HITL Tree Editing + Export
   341→
   342→**Effort**: ~3-5 days. **Dependency**: Phase 2.
   343→
   344→1. **HITL UI**: Display expression tree in criterion review card. Allow editing logic operators, adding/removing atomic nodes, correcting concept IDs and `omop_concept_id`s. Persist changes and re-populate normalized tables.
   345→2. **CIRCE export**: Build `CohortExpression`-style JSON. Use `omop_concept_id` from `atomic_criteria` when present (preferred); fall back to `entity_concept_id` (SNOMED/UMLS) with a note that Usagi review may be needed. Map to OMOP domains and CIRCE attribute filters.
   346→3. **FHIR Group export**: Build `Group` resource with `combinationMethod`, `Group.characteristic` (code = CodeableConcept, value = Quantity or CodeableConcept).
   347→4. **API endpoints**: `GET /protocols/{id}/export/cohort-definition` (CIRCE), `GET /protocols/{id}/export/fhir-group`.
   348→
   349→---
   350→
   351→## 4. Dependency Graph
   352→
   353→```
   354→Phase 1a (dual grounding: MedGemma + OMOP vocabulary)
   355→  └─► Phase 1b (concept IDs in field mappings + accumulate + criterion_id fix)
   356→        │
   357→        ╠══ HARD GATE: Phase 1b must be in QA before any Phase 2+ work begins ══╣
   358→        │
   359→        └─► Phase 2 (expression tree with AND/OR/NOT + normalized tables)
   360→              ├─► Phase 3 (unit normalization)
   361→              ├─► Phase 4 (patient matching — recursive CTE handles any tree shape)
   362→              └─► Phase 5 (HITL + export)
   363→```
   364→
   365→Phases 3, 4, 5 are independent of each other and can be parallelized after Phase 2. The hard gate between 1b and 2 ensures we never build structure over unlinked data.
   366→
   367→---
   368→
   369→## 5. What the Literature Says
   370→
   371→### Criteria2Query 3.0 (Park et al., JBI 2024)
   372→
   373→C2Q 3.0 uses GPT-4 with three prompts (concept extraction, SQL generation, reasoning) to go from free-text criteria to OMOP CDM SQL. Our pipeline has better entity-level grounding (multi-system terminology routing + MedGemma vs. C2Q's single-vocabulary OMOP mapping). The gap: C2Q produces *executable SQL* because it maps entities to OMOP domains and attaches relational operators + values in the same step. We do this in two disconnected steps (ground → field_map) without linking them.
   374→
   375→**Lesson**: Concept ID and relational expression must be a single atomic unit. Phases 1a+1b directly address this.
   376→
   377→### OHDSI Usagi and Athena Vocabulary
   378→
   379→Usagi uses TF-IDF term similarity over OMOP vocabulary tables (`CONCEPT`, `CONCEPT_SYNONYM`) to suggest source-to-standard concept mappings, achieving ~66% automatic match rate. A deep-learning alternative (DEAU) reaches ~77% for conditions, with ~69% overlap between the two — confirming mutual complementarity.
   380→
   381→Usagi is a Java desktop app with no API. For pipeline integration, the recommended approach is to load Athena vocabulary tables into Postgres and replicate Usagi's matching logic in SQL (or use `pg_trgm` for trigram similarity). The community `athena-client` Python SDK (Stanford) or OMOPHub REST API are alternatives.
   382→
   383→**Lesson**: Dual grounding (MedGemma + OMOP vocabulary search) combines the strengths of both paths. Where they agree, confidence is highest.
   384→
   385→### OHDSI CIRCE / Atlas
   386→
   387→CIRCE represents cohort definitions as JSON expression trees with `ConceptSets` referencing OMOP `concept_id`s. Our Phase 2 expression tree is the internal equivalent; Phase 5 export translates to CIRCE format. CIRCE has no inclusion/exclusion distinction — exclusion = 0 occurrences.
   388→
   389→### JMIR 2025 (LLM-based OMOP query generation)
   390→
   391→Found that unit conversion failures (hemoglobin Jaccard=0.00) and incomplete concept hierarchy traversal (diabetes Jaccard=0.04) caused 40% of criteria to fail. Validates Phase 3 (unit normalization) and the future need for OMOP descendant/ancestor traversal.
   392→
   393→### SNOMED CT Entity Linking Challenge (2024)
   394→
   395→Winning approaches used BERT + embedding similarity, achieving good results on ~7K SNOMED concepts. Our MedGemma approach is comparable. The gap is not in entity linking quality but in what we do *after* linking.
   396→
   397→---
   398→
   399→## 6. Success Criteria
   400→
   401→**Phase 1 gate (must pass before Phase 2 begins):**
   402→
   403→1. **Dual grounding**: Every entity has `entity_concept_id` (from ToolUniverse) *and* `omop_concept_id` (from OMOP vocabulary) where matches exist. Reconciliation status stored; disagreements flagged for HITL.
   404→2. **Field mappings carry concept IDs**: `Criteria.conditions.field_mappings[*].entity_concept_id IS NOT NULL` and `omop_concept_id IS NOT NULL` (where OMOP mapping succeeded).
   405→3. **Multi-entity accumulation**: A criterion mentioning N entities has ≥N field mappings, not just the last one.
   406→4. **Criterion-entity linkage is correct**: No field mappings land on wrong criterion records (criterion_id threading verified).
   407→
   408→**Phase 2+ criteria:**
   409→
   410→5. **Expression trees handle AND/OR/NOT**: Criteria with disjunctive or negated logic produce correct tree structures. `composite_criteria` rows with `logic_operator IN ('AND', 'OR', 'NOT')` exist where the criterion text warrants them.
   411→6. **Recursive evaluation is correct**: For test cases with nested AND/OR/NOT, the recursive CTE produces correct boolean results.
   412→7. **Normalized tables populated**: `atomic_criteria` rows exist with `entity_concept_id` and `omop_concept_id` populated, plus `relation_operator` and `value_numeric`/`value_text`.
   413→8. **Join works**: `SELECT * FROM atomic_criteria WHERE omop_concept_id = ? AND relation_operator = ? AND value_numeric = ?` returns correct rows across protocols.
   414→9. **CIRCE export**: Produces valid CIRCE JSON using `omop_concept_id` in concept sets, with nested CriteriaGroups reflecting OR/NOT logic.
   415→10. **End-to-end**: Process protocol → query `atomic_criteria` → get structured, dual-grounded, join-ready rows with correct boolean composition.
   416→
   417→---
   418→

   420→

    33→---
    34→
    35→# Full Plan: Join-Ready Eligibility Criteria (Ultimate Aim)
    36→
    37→This plan takes the repository to its **ultimate aim**: criteria that are fully structured, ontology-grounded, and usable for **patient matching via simple joins**. It incorporates research on Criteria2Query, OMOP CDM / OHDSI CIRCE, FHIR Group (CohortDefinition), and UCUM/LOINC for value and unit normalization.
    38→
    39→---
    40→
    41→## Research Summary (What “Join-Ready” Means)
    42→
    43→- **Criteria2Query (C2Q)** extracts entities, maps them to OMOP concept IDs (via Usagi), and outputs cohort definitions that run against OMOP CDM tables (`CONDITION_OCCURRENCE`, `MEASUREMENT`, `DRUG_EXPOSURE`, etc.). Join-ready means storing the **cohort definition** and running it to produce cohort membership rows. **Usagi** is OHDSI’s tool for mapping source terms to OMOP Vocabulary standard concepts (term-similarity + human review); we run an OMOP mapping path **in parallel** with MedGemma/ToolUniverse to improve reliability.
    44→- **OHDSI / OMOP**: Standard pattern is **COHORT_DEFINITION** (holds `cohort_definition_syntax` e.g. CIRCE JSON) and **COHORT** (cohort_definition_id, subject_id, cohort_start_date, cohort_end_date). CIRCE JSON has PrimaryCriteria, AdditionalCriteria, conceptSets; concept sets use standard concept_id from the OMOP vocabulary (SNOMED, RxNorm, etc. are common sources).
    45→- **FHIR**: **Group** (EBM IG) uses `Group.characteristic` (code = CodeableConcept, value, exclude) and `Group.combinationMethod` (all-of, any-of, at-least, etc.); nested groups give expression trees. **CohortDefinition** and **StudyEligibilityCriteria** profiles standardize trial eligibility.
    46→- **Value/unit**: **UCUM** is the standard for units (NLM provides API); LOINC uses UCUM. For join compatibility with EHRs, store unit_concept_id (e.g. UCUM or OMOP unit concepts) where feasible; value_concept_id for coded values (e.g. positive/negative).
    47→
    48→**Target state:** Internal canonical model = **expression tree** (atomic nodes with entity_concept_id, relation, value, unit; composite nodes with AND/OR/NOT and children). From that we derive: (1) normalized **atomic_criteria** and **composite_criteria** tables for SQL evaluation, (2) **patient_criterion_matches** for fast “find patients for trial” joins, (3) **export** to CIRCE-like JSON and/or FHIR Group for use in OHDSI Atlas or FHIR-based systems.
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→## Current vs. Target (Recap)
    53→
    54→
    55→| Aspect              | Current                                                                      | Target                                                                                                                                     |
    56→| ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
    57→| Field mappings      | entity, relation, value, unit (strings only); no concept IDs                 | entity_concept_id (SNOMED/UMLS/RxNorm), omop_concept_id (when available), relation, value, value_concept_id (optional), unit, unit_concept_id (optional); dual grounding for reliability                       |
    58→| Persist             | One entity’s mappings overwrite criterion’s field_mappings (substring match) | Accumulate all entities’ mappings per criterion_id; each mapping carries concept ID                                                        |
    59→| Criterion structure | Flat Criteria + list of Entities + one field_mappings list                   | Expression tree per criterion (atomic + composite with logic_operator, children)                                                           |
    60→| Schema              | Protocol → CriteriaBatch → Criteria → Entity                                 | Add: atomic_criteria, composite_criteria, criterion_relationships, patient_criterion_matches; optional Criteria.structured_criterion JSONB |
    61→| Matching            | None                                                                         | Precomputed patient_criterion_matches; recursive CTE for composite evaluation                                                              |
    62→| Export              | None                                                                         | CIRCE-like cohort definition and/or FHIR Group (CohortDefinition/StudyEligibilityCriteria)                                                 |
    63→
    64→
    65→---
    66→
    67→## Phase 1a: Parallel Dual Grounding (MedGemma/ToolUniverse + OMOP/Usagi)
    68→
    69→**Goal:** Run two independent concept-mapping paths in parallel so that the final structured criteria are more reliable: (1) MedGemma + ToolUniverse → SNOMED/UMLS/RxNorm; (2) OMOP/Usagi path → OMOP Vocabulary standard concept_id. Reconcile results and store both; use agreement to boost confidence and flag disagreements for HITL.
    70→
    71→1. **Why parallel dual grounding**
    72→   - **MedGemma/ToolUniverse** gives rich, model-driven grounding with multi-terminology (UMLS, SNOMED, RxNorm, LOINC, etc.) and is already in the pipeline.
    73→   - **OMOP/Usagi** gives direct **OMOP standard concept_id**, which is what CIRCE, Atlas, and OMOP CDM cohort evaluation expect. Usagi uses term-similarity over the OMOP Vocabulary and supports human-curated mappings.
    74→   - Running **both** in parallel: when they agree (same concept or OMOP concept consistent with SNOMED), we get high-confidence, join-ready criteria; when they disagree or one fails, we surface it for review and still keep the best available code(s).
    75→
    76→2. **Pipeline shape**
    77→   - After **parse** (entity list per criterion), fork into two concurrent paths:
    78→     - **Branch A (existing):** MedGemma + TerminologyRouter (ToolUniverse) → `entity_concept_id` + `entity_concept_system` (SNOMED, UMLS, RxNorm, etc.).
    79→     - **Branch B (OMOP):** For each entity text (and optional entity_type/domain), call the **OMOP mapping** component → `omop_concept_id` (OMOP standard concept_id).
    80→   - Run A and B in parallel (e.g. same entity list, `asyncio.gather` on two sets of tasks). Then **reconcile** per entity before field-mapping generation and persist.
    81→
    82→3. **OMOP branch implementation options**
    83→   - **Option 1 — Athena / OMOP Vocabulary in DB:** Load OHDSI Athena vocabulary (CONCEPT, CONCEPT_SYNONYM, CONCEPT_RELATIONSHIP) into a database (or use an existing OMOP instance). For each entity text, run a **term-similarity search** (e.g. synonym + concept_name match, or a simple text-similarity score) and return the best **standard_concept** (standard_concept = 'S'). This replicates Usagi’s automatic mapping logic in the pipeline; no Usagi UI, but same vocabulary.
    84→   - **Option 2 — Pre-built Usagi mapping table:** Curators run Usagi offline for a given protocol or term list and export SOURCE_TO_CONCEPT_MAP (or a custom table: source_term → omop_concept_id). The pipeline looks up entity text (or normalized form) in this table. Best when you want human review in Usagi’s UI; pipeline stays read-only for OMOP.
    85→   - **Option 3 — Usagi API (if available):** If OHDSI or a third party exposes a Usagi-compatible API (e.g. term in → suggested concept_id out), call it from the ground node. As of this writing Usagi is a standalone Java app; Option 1 or 2 is the practical approach unless an API is added.
    86→   - **Recommendation:** Implement Option 1 (vocabulary search) first for full automation; add Option 2 (Usagi export import) for protocols where curators have already built Usagi mappings.
    87→
    88→4. **Reconciliation rules**
    89→   - **Both succeed and agree:** OMOP concept_id is consistent with ToolUniverse (e.g. same SNOMED code, or OMOP concept’s source = ToolUniverse code). → Store both; set **grounding_confidence** high; use **omop_concept_id** for CIRCE/atomic_criteria when present.
    90→   - **Both succeed but disagree:** Different concepts (e.g. different SNOMED codes or OMOP vs SNOMED mismatch). → Store both; set a **reconciliation_status** (e.g. `"disagreement"`); **flag for HITL**. Reviewer picks canonical concept; optionally feed back to Usagi mapping table.
    91→   - **Only ToolUniverse succeeds:** Store entity_concept_id; omop_concept_id = null; reconciliation_status = `"omop_missing"`. Export can still use SNOMED/UMLS in concept sets (CIRCE supports source vocabularies); or run a later Usagi pass to backfill omop_concept_id.
    92→   - **Only OMOP succeeds:** Store omop_concept_id; entity_concept_id can be derived from OMOP vocabulary (concept_code + vocabulary_id) or left null. reconciliation_status = `"tooluniverse_missing"`. Prefer omop_concept_id for CIRCE.
    93→   - **Neither succeeds:** Leave both null; flag for expert review as today.
    94→
    95→5. **Schema extensions**
    96→   - **Entity:** Add `omop_concept_id: str | None` (OMOP standard concept_id). Optionally add `reconciliation_status: str | None` (e.g. `"agree"`, `"disagreement"`, `"omop_missing"`, `"tooluniverse_missing"`).
    97→   - **Field mappings (conditions JSON):** Each mapping includes `entity_concept_id`, `entity_concept_system` (from ToolUniverse) and `omop_concept_id` (from OMOP branch) when present.
    98→   - **atomic_criteria (Phase 3):** Include both `entity_concept_id` (for FHIR/generic joins) and `omop_concept_id` (for CIRCE/OMOP). CIRCE export uses `omop_concept_id` when not null.
    99→
   100→6. **Key files**
   101→   - New: **OMOP mapping client** (e.g. `protocol_processor/tools/omop_mapper.py` or `usagi_client.py`): input entity text (+ domain), output suggested omop_concept_id (and optional match score). Implement via Athena DB query or lookup table.
   102→   - [ground.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/ground.py): Invoke Branch A (existing) and Branch B (OMOP) in parallel; merge results and run reconciliation; pass merged result (both IDs + reconciliation_status) into field_mapping generation and persist.
   103→   - [shared/models.py](libs/shared/src/shared/models.py): Entity.omop_concept_id, Entity.reconciliation_status (optional). Migration in api-service.
   104→
   105→**Deliverable:** Each entity has (when available) both ToolUniverse concept (entity_concept_id + system) and OMOP concept (omop_concept_id); reconciliation status stored; disagreements and single-source results flagged for HITL. Most reliable structured criteria come from entities where both paths agree.
   106→
   107→---
   108→
   109→## Phase 1: Concept-Grounded Field Mappings (Foundation)
   110→
   111→**Goal:** Every field mapping row is join-ready: it carries entity concept ID(s) from dual grounding and is tied to the correct criterion without overwriting other entities’ mappings.
   112→
   113→1. **Schema and generation**
   114→  - In [field_mapper.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/field_mapper.py): Extend `FieldMappingItem` (and the dict returned) with:
   115→    - `entity_concept_id: str | None` (SNOMED preferred when available for conditions/measurements; else UMLS CUI)
   116→    - `entity_concept_system: str | None` (e.g. "SNOMED", "UMLS", "RxNorm")
   117→    - `omop_concept_id: str | None` (from Phase 1a OMOP branch when available)
   118→    - Optionally `entity_id: str | None` for traceability to the Entity record; `reconciliation_status: str | None` when dual grounding is used.
   119→  - When calling `generate_field_mappings`, pass the **reconciled** entity (both ToolUniverse and OMOP fields from Phase 1a); set these on each mapping item in the returned list.
   120→2. **Persist: accumulate by criterion_id**
   121→  - In [persist.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/persist.py):
   122→    - Stop using substring match to find criterion. Use **criterion_id** from the grounding payload (entity_items already carry `criterion_id` from [parse.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/parse.py); ensure ground node passes it through to each result so persist receives it).
   123→    - For each criterion_id: **append** this entity’s field_mappings (with entity_concept_id, entity_concept_system, omop_concept_id, entity_id) to existing `conditions["field_mappings"]` instead of overwriting. If no field_mappings key exists, create it as a list; then extend.
   124→  - In [ground.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/ground.py): When building grounding_results for persist, include `criterion_id` (and later entity_id) and the reconciled concept IDs from Phase 1a so persist can group by criterion and append.
   125→3. **API and UI**
   126→  - [reviews.py](services/api-service/src/api_service/reviews.py): CriterionResponse already returns `conditions`; ensure field_mappings in conditions include the new keys (entity_concept_id, entity_concept_system, omop_concept_id, entity_id, reconciliation_status when present).
   127→  - [useReviews.ts](apps/hitl-ui/src/hooks/useReviews.ts): Extend `FieldMapping` type with `entity_concept_id?`, `entity_concept_system?`, `omop_concept_id?`, `entity_id?`, `reconciliation_status?`.
   128→  - [FieldMappingBadges.tsx](apps/hitl-ui/src/components/FieldMappingBadges.tsx) and structured editor: Display (and allow editing) entity_concept_id, omop_concept_id, and reconciliation status so reviewers can resolve disagreements and correct.
   129→
   130→**Deliverable:** Criteria.conditions.field_mappings = list of { entity, relation, value, unit, entity_concept_id, entity_concept_system?, omop_concept_id?, entity_id?, reconciliation_status? }, accumulated across all entities for that criterion. Downstream can join on entity_concept_id or omop_concept_id + relation + value; CIRCE export prefers omop_concept_id when available.
   131→
   132→---
   133→
   134→## Phase 2: Expression Tree (Atomic + Composite)
   135→
   136→**Goal:** Each criterion has a canonical expression tree: atomic nodes (entity_concept_id, relation, value, unit, negation) and composite nodes (logic_operator, children). Stored in DB for evaluation and export.
   137→
   138→1. **Canonical tree format (align with [output_criteria.md](instructions/Refactoring/output_criteria.md))**
   139→  - **Atomic node:** criterion_id (stable id, e.g. criterion_id + "_a", "_b"), criterion_type: "atomic", entity_concept_id, entity_concept_system, **omop_concept_id** (when available from Phase 1a), entity_term, relation, value, unit, negation, (optional value_concept_id, unit_concept_id).
   140→  - **Composite node:** criterion_id, criterion_type: "composite", logic_operator: "AND" | "OR" | "NOT", children: list of nodes (atomic or composite).
   141→2. **Building the tree**
   142→  - **Option A (pragmatic):** One composite root per criterion with logic_operator "AND" and one atomic child per field mapping (from Phase 1). No nested OR/NOT unless we add logic detection later. Simple to implement and already join-ready.
   143→  - **Option B (richer):** New “structure” node or post-processing step: use LLM or rules to parse criterion text + field_mappings into an explicit tree (e.g. “SpO2 > 93% AND (respiratory rate < 24 OR on supplemental oxygen)”). More accurate for complex criteria but higher effort.
   144→  - **Recommendation:** Start with Option A; add Option B in a later iteration (e.g. Gemini structured output for logic_operator and grouping per criterion).
   145→3. **Storage**
   146→  - Add column `structured_criterion` (JSONB) to **Criteria** table. Store the root node of the expression tree (JSON matches the format above). Migrate existing criteria: backfill from current field_mappings (one AND composite with atomics from each mapping that has entity_concept_id).
   147→  - **Alternative:** New table `criterion_expression` (criterion_id PK, expression_json JSONB) if you prefer not to widen Criteria.
   148→4. **Pipeline integration**
   149→  - **Option 1:** New pipeline node **structure** after **persist**: reads Criteria + Entities + conditions.field_mappings for the batch, builds tree (Option A or B), writes structured_criterion.
   150→  - **Option 2:** Same logic in a **batch job** or **API-triggered job** that runs after grounding (e.g. on “approve batch” or on a schedule). Prefer Option 1 if you want every new protocol to have a tree without a separate job.
   151→
   152→**Deliverable:** Every criterion has structured_criterion (JSONB) with at least AND-of-atomics; each atomic has entity_concept_id, relation, value, unit.
   153→
   154→---
   155→
   156→## Phase 3: Normalized Schema (atomic_criteria, composite_criteria, criterion_relationships)
   157→
   158→**Goal:** Flatten the expression tree into normalized tables so that SQL can join on entity_concept_id and so composite evaluation can use parent/child links.
   159→
   160→1. **New tables (Alembic migrations in api-service)**
   161→  - **atomic_criteria**
   162→    - id (PK), criterion_id (FK to criteria.id), protocol_id (trial_id; from criteria.batch_id → criteria_batch.protocol_id),
   163→    - inclusion_exclusion ("inclusion" | "exclusion"), entity_concept_id (NOT NULL for join flexibility), entity_concept_system, **omop_concept_id** (nullable; prefer for CIRCE/OMOP export when present), entity_domain (condition, measurement, drug, procedure, demographics, etc.),
   164→    - relation_operator, value_numeric, value_text, value_concept_id, unit_concept_id, unit_text, negation (boolean),
   165→    - temporal_constraint (JSONB), original_text, confidence_score, human_verified, human_modified, created_at, updated_at.
   166→    - Indexes: (protocol_id, inclusion_exclusion), (entity_concept_id), (omop_concept_id), (criterion_id).
   167→  - **composite_criteria**
   168→    - id (PK), criterion_id (FK), protocol_id, inclusion_exclusion, logic_operator ("AND" | "OR" | "NOT"), parent_criterion_id (nullable; for nesting),
   169→    - original_text, human_verified, created_at, updated_at.
   170→    - Indexes: (protocol_id), (parent_criterion_id).
   171→  - **criterion_relationships**
   172→    - parent_criterion_id, child_criterion_id, child_sequence (int; order of children),
   173→    - PK (parent_criterion_id, child_criterion_id).
   174→    - Links composite → atomic or composite → composite.
   175→  - **SQLModel classes** in [shared/models.py](libs/shared/src/shared/models.py) (or api-service if models stay there) and use in persist/structure logic.
   176→2. **Population**
   177→  - When building the expression tree (Phase 2), also:
   178→    - Insert into **atomic_criteria** one row per atomic node (criterion_id = that node’s id; protocol_id from batch; entity_concept_id, relation_operator, value_*, unit_*, etc. from node).
   179→    - Insert into **composite_criteria** one row per composite node.
   180→    - Insert into **criterion_relationships** for each (parent, child, sequence).
   181→  - **Idempotency:** On re-run (e.g. re-grounding or re-structure), delete atomic_criteria/composite_criteria/criterion_relationships for affected criterion_ids then re-insert.
   182→
   183→**Deliverable:** Normalized tables populated from expression tree; queries can join on atomic_criteria.entity_concept_id and use criterion_relationships for recursive composite evaluation.
   184→
   185→---
   186→
   187→## Phase 4: Value and Unit Normalization (Optional but Recommended)
   188→
   189→**Goal:** Where possible, store unit_concept_id (e.g. UCUM) and value_concept_id (for coded values) so EHR systems using the same vocabularies can join reliably.
   190→
   191→- **Units:** Use NLM UCUM API or a UCUM lookup table to map free-text unit (e.g. "%", "mg/dL") to a standard code; store in atomic_criteria.unit_concept_id (or a separate unit vocabulary table). If OMOP is the target, use OMOP’s unit concept_id from the vocabulary.
   192→- **Values:** For categorical values (e.g. "positive", "negative", "confirmed"), consider value set or SNOMED/LOINC lookup and store value_concept_id. For numeric thresholds, value_numeric + unit_concept_id is sufficient.
   193→- **Scope:** Can be a follow-up task after Phase 3; document in API/schema that unit_concept_id and value_concept_id are optional.
   194→
   195→---
   196→
   197→## Phase 5: Patient Criterion Matches (Fast Matching)
   198→
   199→**Goal:** Table and pipeline to precompute, per patient, whether each atomic and composite criterion is satisfied, so “find patients for trial” is a simple join.
   200→
   201→1. **Schema**
   202→  - **patient_criterion_matches**
   203→    - patient_id, criterion_id (references the atomic or composite criterion_id used in atomic_criteria/composite_criteria),
   204→    - matches (boolean), evaluated_at (timestamp), evaluation_data (JSONB; e.g. actual value used, source).
   205→    - PK (patient_id, criterion_id). Index (patient_id, criterion_id, matches) for fast “all inclusion matched and no exclusion matched” queries.
   206→2. **Assumptions**
   207→  - A **patient** data model exists somewhere (e.g. in this repo or in an external EHR/OMOP database). If in-repo: minimal **patients** table (patient_id, plus demographics/observations as needed for evaluation). If external: evaluation runs in that DB or via an adapter that reads patient data and writes back to this repo’s patient_criterion_matches.
   208→  - **Evaluation logic:**
   209→    - **Phase 1 (atomics):** For each atomic_criteria row, evaluate against patient data (e.g. age >= value_numeric, or measurement value comparison). Insert/update patient_criterion_matches (patient_id, criterion_id, matches, evaluation_data).
   210→    - **Phase 2 (composites):** Recursive CTE (as in [output_criteria.md](instructions/Refactoring/output_criteria.md)): compute composite result from children’s matches (AND/OR/NOT) and insert into patient_criterion_matches.
   211→  - **When to run:** On patient data change (or batch refresh); or on criteria change (re-evaluate all patients for that trial). Can be a separate service or batch job that reads atomic_criteria/composite_criteria and patient data and writes patient_criterion_matches.
   212→3. **“Find patients for trial” query**
   213→  - For a given protocol_id (trial): exclude patients who fail any root-level inclusion criterion or pass any root-level exclusion criterion (using patient_criterion_matches + composite_criteria/criterion_relationships to identify root criteria). Example pattern in output_criteria.md (NOT EXISTS failed inclusion, NOT EXISTS passed exclusion).
   214→
   215→**Deliverable:** patient_criterion_matches populated; API or query pattern that returns list of patient_ids eligible for a trial.
   216→
   217→---
   218→
   219→## Phase 6: HITL for Expression Tree
   220→
   221→**Goal:** Reviewers can see and approve/modify the expression tree (and thus the atomic/composite rows); corrections feed back for ML and consistency.
   222→
   223→- **UI:** In the criterion review card, show the expression tree (e.g. AND of atomics with entity_concept_id, relation, value). Allow “Modify structure” to change logic_operator (AND/OR) or to fix/remove atomic nodes. Persist changes to Criteria.structured_criterion and re-populate atomic_criteria/composite_criteria/criterion_relationships.
   224→- **Audit:** Log modifications in Review/AuditLog (before_value / after_value = tree snapshot). Optionally store in **criterion_corrections** (as in output_criteria.md) for ML training signals.
   225→
   226→---
   227→
   228→## Phase 7: Export (CIRCE-Like and FHIR Group)
   229→
   230→**Goal:** Emit cohort definitions so external systems (OHDSI Atlas, FHIR-based matchers) can run the same eligibility logic.
   231→
   232→1. **CIRCE-like JSON**
   233→  - Build **CohortExpression**-style JSON: conceptSets (list of concept set expressions). Prefer **omop_concept_id** from atomic_criteria when present (direct OMOP standard concept); otherwise use entity_concept_id (SNOMED/UMLS) and note in export that OMOP mapping may need Usagi review. PrimaryCriteria (cohort entry, e.g. “person in CDM”), AdditionalCriteria (inclusion rules: each rule references concept sets and attributes; exclusion = 0 occurrences). Map our atomic_criteria to OMOP domain (condition, measurement, drug_exposure, etc.) and relation/value to CIRCE attribute filters where possible. Output as JSON file or API response.
   234→  - **Reference:** [CirceR](https://ohdsi.github.io/CirceR/), [circe-be](https://github.com/OHDSI/circe-be). Our internal tree is the source of truth; export is a translation layer. Dual grounding (Phase 1a) maximizes the number of criteria with omop_concept_id for CIRCE.
   235→2. **FHIR Group (CohortDefinition / StudyEligibilityCriteria)**
   236→  - Build **Group** resource: combinationMethod = "all-of" (for top-level AND); characteristics = list of Group.characteristic (code = CodeableConcept with system/code from entity_concept_id, value[x] = Quantity or CodeableConcept, exclude = for exclusion criteria). For nested OR, use a nested Group with combinationMethod "any-of" and reference it. Align with [HL7 EBM IG Group](https://build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/ebm/en/group.html) and StudyEligibilityCriteria profile.
   237→3. **API**
   238→  - e.g. `GET /protocols/{protocol_id}/export/cohort-definition` (CIRCE-like JSON) and `GET /protocols/{protocol_id}/export/fhir-group` (FHIR Group JSON). Both read from atomic_criteria/composite_criteria and Criteria.structured_criterion.
   239→
   240→**Deliverable:** Export endpoints or scripts producing CIRCE-like and FHIR Group definitions for each protocol.
   241→
   242→---
   243→
   244→## Implementation Order and Dependencies
   245→
   246→```mermaid
   247→flowchart TD
   248→    P1a[Phase 1a: Parallel dual grounding]
   249→    P1[Phase 1: Concept-ground field mappings]
   250→    P2[Phase 2: Expression tree]
   251→    P3[Phase 3: Normalized schema]
   252→    P4[Phase 4: Value/unit normalization]
   253→    P5[Phase 5: patient_criterion_matches]
   254→    P6[Phase 6: HITL tree UI]
   255→    P7[Phase 7: Export CIRCE/FHIR]
   256→
   257→    P1a --> P1
   258→    P1 --> P2
   259→    P2 --> P3
   260→    P3 --> P5
   261→    P3 --> P7
   262→    P4 -.-> P3
   263→    P3 --> P6
   264→```
   265→
   266→- **Phase 1a** (parallel MedGemma/ToolUniverse + OMOP/Usagi) feeds reconciled concept IDs into Phase 1; agreement between the two paths yields the most reliable structured criteria.
   267→- **Phase 1** is required for join-ready mappings and for Phase 2 input.
   268→- **Phase 2** produces the tree; Phase 3 consumes it.
   269→- **Phase 4** can be done in parallel or after Phase 3.
   270→- **Phase 5** depends on Phase 3 and on a defined patient data source.
   271→- **Phase 6** depends on Phase 2/3 so the UI can display and edit the tree.
   272→- **Phase 7** depends on Phase 3 (normalized + tree).
   273→
   274→---
   275→
   276→## Key Files (Summary)
   277→
   278→
   279→| Area                               | Files                                                                                                                                                                                                |
   280→| ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
   281→| Parallel dual grounding            | New: `protocol_processor/tools/omop_mapper.py` (or usagi_client); [ground.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/ground.py) (invoke both branches, reconcile); [shared/models.py](libs/shared/src/shared/models.py) (Entity.omop_concept_id, reconciliation_status) |

   283→| Persist accumulate by criterion_id | [ground.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/ground.py), [persist.py](services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/persist.py)                   |
   284→| Expression tree build + store      | New: `protocol_processor/tools/structure_builder.py` or node `structure.py`; [Criteria model](libs/shared/src/shared/models.py) add structured_criterion                                             |
   285→| Normalized tables                  | New Alembic migration; [shared/models.py](libs/shared/src/shared/models.py) (AtomicCriterion, CompositeCriterion, CriterionRelationship, PatientCriterionMatch)                                      |
   286→| Population from tree               | Same as structure step or separate job in api-service                                                                                                                                                |
   287→| Patient evaluation                 | New service or script: read atomic_criteria, run Phase 1+2 SQL, write patient_criterion_matches                                                                                                      |
   288→| HITL tree UI                       | [CriterionCard.tsx](apps/hitl-ui/src/components/CriterionCard.tsx), new tree editor component, API PATCH for structured_criterion                                                                    |
   289→| Export                             | New API routes in api-service, e.g. [protocols.py](services/api-service/src/api_service/protocols.py) or new export.py                                                                               |
   290→
   291→
   292→---
   293→
   294→## Success Criteria
   295→
   296→- **Dual grounding:** MedGemma/ToolUniverse and OMOP/Usagi (or Athena vocabulary) run in parallel; results are reconciled and stored (entity_concept_id + omop_concept_id); disagreements and single-source results are flagged for HITL. Where both agree, structured criteria are highest confidence.
   297→- Every criterion has field_mappings with entity_concept_id (and omop_concept_id when available); mappings are accumulated per criterion.
   298→- Every criterion has an expression tree (at least AND-of-atomics) in structured_criterion and corresponding rows in atomic_criteria (and composite_criteria + criterion_relationships where composite); atomic_criteria carries both entity_concept_id and omop_concept_id where applicable.
   299→- “Find patients for trial” is implementable as a join on patient_criterion_matches and atomic_criteria/composite_criteria.
   300→- Export produces CIRCE-like JSON (preferring omop_concept_id for concept sets when present) and FHIR Group that represent the same eligibility logic for use in OHDSI or FHIR-based systems.
   301→
   302→

     1→
     2→# Code review report: ElixirTrials / MedGemma Hackathon
     3→
     4→## Executive summary
     5→
     6→Review of the protocol-processor-service, api-service, shared models, and related code found **several bugs**, **logical flaws**, **inconsistencies**, and **duplicated patterns**. Items are grouped by severity.
     7→
     8→---
     9→
    10→## 1. Bugs
    11→
    12→### 1.1 Dead code in ground node (ground.py:389)
    13→
    14→```python
    15→entity.get("criterion_text") or entity.get("text", "")
    16→```
    17→
    18→This expression is evaluated and discarded. It has no effect. If it was meant for logging or passing to audit, the result should be used. Otherwise it should be removed.
    19→
    20→---
    21→
    22→### 1.2 Persist: no batch validation on criterion_id lookup (persist.py:139–140)
    23→
    24→When `criterion_id` is provided, `session.get(Criteria, criterion_id)` is used without checking that the criterion belongs to the batch:
    25→
    26→```python
    27→if criterion_id:
    28→    criterion = session.get(Criteria, criterion_id)
    29→```
    30→
    31→If a criterion_id from another batch is passed (e.g. cross-protocol), mappings would be written to the wrong criterion.
    32→
    33→**Recommendation:** Add a batch check and fall through to substring search if it fails:
    34→
    35→```python
    36→if criterion_id:
    37→    criterion = session.get(Criteria, criterion_id)
    38→    if criterion and criterion.batch_id != batch_id:
    39→        criterion = None
    40→```
    41→
    42→---
    43→
    44→### 1.3 Unit normalizer imported but never used (structure_builder.py)
    45→
    46→`normalize_unit` and `normalize_value` are imported but never called. `_create_atomic_from_mapping` sets `unit_text` but not `unit_concept_id`, despite Phase 3 (Gap 7) expecting unit normalization.
    47→
    48→**Recommendation:** Either call `normalize_unit(fm.get("unit"))` in `_create_atomic_from_mapping` and set `unit_concept_id`, or remove the unused import until Phase 3 is implemented.
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→### 1.4 Integrity check misses multi-terminology grounding (integrity.py:141–151)
    53→
    54→`_check_ungrounded_entities` only checks `umls_cui` and `snomed_code`:
    55→
    56→```python
    57→stmt = select(Entity).where(
    58→    col(Entity.umls_cui).is_(None),
    59→    col(Entity.snomed_code).is_(None),
    60→    col(Entity.grounding_error).is_(None),
    61→)
    62→```
    63→
    64→Entities grounded via RxNorm, ICD-10, LOINC, HPO, or OMOP are incorrectly flagged as “ungrounded” because the other terminology columns are ignored.
    65→
    66→---
    67→
    68→## 2. Inconsistencies
    69→
    70→### 2.1 GEMINI_MODEL_NAME defaults
    71→
    72→Different parts of the pipeline use different defaults:
    73→
    74→| Module                | Default             |
    75→|-----------------------|---------------------|
    76→| parse.py              | `gemini-2.5-flash`  |
    77→| gemini_extractor.py   | `gemini-2.5-flash`  |
    78→| criterion_rerun.py    | `gemini-2.5-flash`  |
    79→| field_mapper.py       | `gemini-2.0-flash`  |
    80→| structure_builder.py  | `gemini-2.0-flash`  |
    81→| entity_decomposer.py  | `gemini-2.0-flash`  |
    82→| medgemma_decider.py   | `gemini-2.0-flash`  |
    83→
    84→`.env.example` uses `gemini-2.5-flash`.
    85→
    86→**Recommendation:** Standardize on one default (e.g. `gemini-2.5-flash`) and use it everywhere via env config.
    87→
    88→---
    89→
    90→### 2.2 Outdated docstrings
    91→
    92→- **state.py:1** – “5-node” vs actual 6-node pipeline (ingest, extract, parse, ground, persist, structure).
    93→- **persist.py:3–4** – Still describes persist as the “final node” and the flow as ending at persist; structure now runs after persist.
    94→- **persist.py:1–4** – Comment flow does not mention the structure node.
    95→
    96→---
    97→
    98→### 2.3 EntityResponse missing Phase 1a fields (entities.py)
    99→
   100→`Entity` has `omop_concept_id` and `reconciliation_status`, but `EntityResponse` and `_entity_to_response` do not expose them. The HITL UI cannot show dual-grounding and reconciliation status.
   101→
   102→---
   103→
   104→## 3. Duplicated logic
   105→
   106→### 3.1 Repeated Gemini initialization
   107→
   108→Multiple tools create Gemini clients with similar code:
   109→
   110→- `field_mapper.py` – ChatGoogleGenerativeAI + `with_structured_output`
   111→- `structure_builder.py` – Same pattern in two places
   112→- `entity_decomposer.py` – Same pattern
   113→- `medgemma_decider.py` – Same pattern in two places
   114→
   115→**Recommendation:** Extract a shared helper in a module like `protocol_processor.tools.llm_utils` (or reuse `libs/inference`) to create structured-output clients and reduce repetition.
   116→
   117→---
   118→
   119→### 3.2 Structured result handling
   120→
   121→Several tools use the same handling pattern:
   122→
   123→```python
   124→if isinstance(result, dict):
   125→    response = FieldMappingResponse.model_validate(result)
   126→else:
   127→    response = result  # type: ignore[assignment]
   128→```
   129→
   130→This appears in field_mapper, structure_builder, and medgemma_decider. It could be centralized in a small helper to avoid drift.
   131→
   132→---
   133→
   134→## 4. Subtle / possible logical flaws
   135→
   136→### 4.1 _find_last_composite_id may be ambiguous (structure_builder.py:299–322)
   137→
   138→When building the expression tree, multiple `CompositeCriterion` rows can share the same `logic_operator` (e.g. two OR nodes). The code resolves the child by “most recent” via `created_at.desc()`. If two composites share the same timestamp, the result may be non-deterministic.
   139→
   140→**Recommendation:** Have `_build_tree_from_logic` return the created composite’s ID and pass it to the parent instead of looking it up. That removes the need for `_find_last_composite_id` and avoids ordering ambiguity.
   141→
   142→---
   143→
   144→### 4.2 _log_grounding_audit `entity` parameter unused (ground.py:111–134)
   145→
   146→`_log_grounding_audit` takes `entity` but only uses `result` for details. The parameter is redundant and could be removed unless it’s planned for future use.
   147→
   148→---
   149→
   150→### 4.3 Exception handling in get_graph (graph.py:141)
   151→
   152→```python
   153→except (KeyError, Exception):
   154→```
   155→
   156→Catching `Exception` hides unexpected failures (e.g. DB connection issues). A narrower exception set would make debugging easier and keep the “fallback when DATABASE_URL is missing” behavior clear.
   157→
   158→---
   159→
   160→## 5. Minor / cleanup
   161→
   162→### 5.1 Persist `all_failed` logic
   163→
   164→The condition is:
   165→
   166→```python
   167→all_failed = (
   168→    len(grounding_results) > 0
   169→    and len(successful) == 0
   170→    and len(accumulated_errors) > 0
   171→)
   172→```
   173→
   174→This correctly requires `accumulated_errors > 0`, so consent-only or expert-review-only runs are not treated as total failures. No change needed.
   175→
   176→---
   177→
   178→### 5.2 system_field_map in persist.py (65–87)
   179→
   180→The `system_field_map` chain can be simplified, e.g.:
   181→
   182→```python
   183→field = system_field_map.get(selected_system)
   184→if field and hasattr(Entity, field):
   185→    setattr(entity, field, selected_code)  # or similar
   186→```
   187→
   188→This reduces repetition and the chance of missing a mapping.
   189→
   190→---
   191→
   192→## 6. Recommended priority order
   193→
   194→| # | Issue                              | Severity | Effort |
   195→|---|------------------------------------|----------|--------|
   196→| 1 | Integrity check misses multi-term  | High     | Low    |
   197→| 2 | Persist batch validation for ID   | High     | Low    |
   198→| 3 | Unit normalizer not used          | Medium   | Low    |
   199→| 4 | EntityResponse OMOP fields        | Medium   | Low    |
   200→| 5 | Dead code in ground node          | Low      | Trivial |
   201→| 6 | GEMINI_MODEL_NAME consistency     | Low      | Low    |
   202→| 7 | Docstring updates                 | Low      | Trivial |
   203→| 8 | Refactor _find_last_composite_id   | Low      | Medium  |
```

> AGENT

The `test_get_graph_singleton` test is failing because narrowing to `OSError` doesn't catch `psycopg.OperationalError`. Let me check the exception hierarchy.

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01DUSUdMRbh8jJt9sXcdRPhE
```json
{
  "command": "uv run python -c \"import psycopg; print(psycopg.OperationalError.__mro__)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Check psycopg.OperationalError MRO"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01DUSUdMRbh8jJt9sXcdRPhE
```
(<class 'psycopg.OperationalError'>, <class 'psycopg.DatabaseError'>, <class 'psycopg.Error'>, <class 'Exception'>, <class 'BaseException'>, <class 'object'>)
```

> AGENT

`psycopg.OperationalError` inherits from `Exception`, not `OSError`. The original `except (KeyError, Exception)` was there for a reason — it needs to catch database connection failures too. The review's suggestion to narrow the catch is valid in principle, but the right scope is `KeyError` (env var missing) + `psycopg.Error` (DB connection issues), not `OSError`. However, importing psycopg just for this would add a dependency. The simplest correct fix is `except Exception` with a clear comment:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01HFzaEzHTTSrZUy17HjWU5w
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/graph.py",
  "old_string": "        except (KeyError, OSError):\n            # DATABASE_URL not set (e.g. unit tests) — compile without checkpointer\n            checkpointer = None",
  "new_string": "        except Exception:\n            # KeyError: DATABASE_URL not set; psycopg.OperationalError: DB\n            # unreachable (e.g. unit tests). Compile without checkpointer.\n            checkpointer = None"
}
```

> TOOL

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive view. Let me create a detailed summary document of the findings:

## Comprehensive Phase 4: Patient Matching Infrastructure Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the medgemma-hackathon codebase, here's what exists and what needs to be built for Phase 4:

---

## 1. EXISTING INFRASTRUCTURE (What's Already Built)

### 1.1 Models & Database Schema

**✅ ALREADY EXIST (in `/libs/shared/src/shared/models.py`):**

- **`Entity` model** (lines 114-142): Fully grounded with:
  - Multi-terminology codes: `umls_cui`, `snomed_code`, `rxnorm_code`, `icd10_code`, `loinc_code`, `hpo_code`
  - **Phase 1a columns** (already added): `omop_concept_id`, `reconciliation_status`
  - Indexes on `omop_concept_id` for join performance

- **`AtomicCriterion` model** (lines 184-233): **PHASE 2 COMPLETE**
  - Stores: `criterion_id` (FK to criteria), `protocol_id`, `inclusion_exclusion`
  - Entity grounding: `entity_concept_id`, `entity_concept_system`, `omop_concept_id`
  - Threshold data: `relation_operator`, `value_numeric`, `value_text`, `value_concept_id`
  - Units: `unit_text`, `unit_concept_id` (OMOP UCUM mapping)
  - Negation, temporal constraints, confidence scores
  - Indexes on `(protocol_id, inclusion_exclusion)`, `entity_concept_id`, `omop_concept_id`

- **`CompositeCriterion` model** (lines 235-258): **PHASE 2 COMPLETE**
  - Stores: `logic_operator` (AND/OR/NOT), `parent_criterion_id` (for nesting)
  - Includes `inclusion_exclusion` for tracking inclusion vs exclusion rules

- **`CriterionRelationship` model** (lines 260-280): **PHASE 2 COMPLETE**
  - Links parent composites to child atomics/composites
  - `child_type` discriminator for polymorphic FK
  - `child_sequence` preserves order for AND/OR evaluation

- **`Criteria` model** (lines 86-112):
  - `structured_criterion` (JSON): Stores full expression tree (Phase 2)

### 1.2 OMOP Vocabulary Data

**✅ LOADED AT `/data/omop_vocab/`:**
- `CONCEPT.csv` (7.3M rows): Standard concepts with `domain_id`, `vocabulary_id`, `standard_concept` flag
- `CONCEPT_SYNONYM.csv` (4.2M rows): Synonym mappings
- `CONCEPT_RELATIONSHIP.csv` (43M rows): Ancestor/descendant and mapping relationships
- `CONCEPT_ANCESTOR.csv` (79M rows): Hierarchy data for traversal
- `DRUG_STRENGTH.csv` (3M rows): Drug quantity/strength mappings
- Load script at `load_omop_vocab.sql`

### 1.3 Dual Grounding Pipeline

**✅ PHASE 1a & 1b COMPLETE:**

- **`omop_mapper.py`** (lines 1-436): Full OMOP vocabulary lookup tool
  - Queries `CONCEPT` and `CONCEPT_SYNONYM` with domain filtering
  - Fuzzy scoring via `SequenceMatcher` (min 0.3 threshold)
  - Returns `OmopLookupResult` with concept_id, vocabulary_id, domain_id, match_score
  - Dedicated SQLAlchemy engine (OMOP_VOCAB_URL) separate from main DB

- **Entity threading through pipeline**: 
  - `parse.py`: Associates entities with `criterion_id`
  - `ground.py`: Runs MedGemma + ToolUniverse + OMOP in parallel; reconciles results
  - `persist.py`: Stores grounding results with `omop_concept_id`, `reconciliation_status`

### 1.4 Field Mapping Concept Grounding

**✅ PHASE 1b COMPLETE:**

- **`field_mapper.py`**: Generates mappings with:
  - `entity_concept_id`, `entity_concept_system` (from ToolUniverse)
  - `omop_concept_id` (from OMOP mapper)
  - Relation operators, values, units

- **`persist.py` (_find_criterion_and_update_mappings, lines 108-179)**:
  - Uses `criterion_id` for direct lookup (Gap 3 fix)
  - **ACCUMULATES** field_mappings via `extend()` instead of overwriting (Gap 2 fix)
  - Each mapping carries concept IDs for join-readiness (Gap 1 fix)

### 1.5 Expression Tree & Structure Phase

**✅ PHASE 2 COMPLETE:**

- **`structure_builder.py`** (464 lines): Full expression tree builder
  - Two-pass approach:
    - Pass 1: LLM (Gemini) detects AND/OR/NOT logic from criterion text
    - Pass 2: Fallback to AND-of-all-atomics if LLM fails
  - Creates `AtomicCriterion` records for each field_mapping
  - Creates `CompositeCriterion` + `CriterionRelationship` for tree structure
  - Stores `StructuredCriterionTree` JSONB on `Criteria.structured_criterion`

- **`structure.py` node** (206 lines): Pipeline integration
  - Async processing with `asyncio.Semaphore(4)` for LLM concurrency
  - Processes all criteria in a batch after persist node
  - Creates atomic/composite DB records via structure_builder

- **Alembic migration `42_01_add_expression_tree_tables.py`** (165 lines):
  - Creates atomic_criteria, composite_criteria, criterion_relationships tables
  - Adds structured_criterion JSONB column to criteria
  - Creates proper indexes for join performance

### 1.6 Unit & Value Normalization

**✅ PHASE 3 COMPLETE:**

- **`ucum_mappings.yaml`** (277 lines): Static UCUM → OMOP unit_concept_id mappings
  - ~50 common units (time, mass, volume, concentration, pressure, rate)
  - Aliases for each unit (e.g., "years", "yr", "yrs" → 9448)

- **`unit_normalizer.py`** (241 lines):
  - `normalize_unit()`: Maps free-text units to OMOP unit_concept_id
  - `normalize_value()`: Maps categorical values (e.g., "positive" → SNOMED concept)
  - Ordinal scale support (ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, etc.) with unit_concept_id=8527 ({score})
  - `normalize_ordinal_value()`: Entity-context-aware grade normalization

- **Alembic migration `43_01_add_unit_value_concept_ids.py`**:
  - Adds `unit_concept_id` and `value_concept_id` columns to atomic_criteria

### 1.7 Evaluation Logic Foundation

**From `output_criteria.md` (embedded in instructions):**
- Recursive CTE pattern documented:
  - Base case: Atomic criteria evaluation (with operator comparison)
  - Recursive case: Composite evaluation using `BOOL_AND`, `BOOL_OR`, `NOT`
  - Supports nested AND/OR/NOT of arbitrary depth

---

## 2. WHAT'S MISSING FOR PHASE 4: Patient Matching Infrastructure

### 2.1 Patient Model & Data

**❌ NOT YET BUILT:**
- No `Patient` table exists
- No assumed patient data source (OMOP CDM instance expected, not included)
- No patient demographics/observation tables

**What's needed:**
- Either load real OMOP CDM patient data, OR
- Create mock patient table with `patient_id`, `birth_date`, and observation records

### 2.2 Patient Criterion Matches Table

**❌ NOT YET BUILT:**
```python
class PatientCriterionMatch(SQLModel, table=True):
    """Precomputed boolean match of each patient against each criterion."""
    patient_id: str = Field(foreign_key="patient.id", primary_key=True)
    criterion_id: str = Field(primary_key=True)  # atomic or composite criterion_id
    matches: bool  # evaluation result
    evaluated_at: datetime
    evaluation_data: dict  # actual values used in evaluation
    
    Indexes: (patient_id, criterion_id, matches) for fast cohort queries
```

**Alembic migration needed** in `api-service/alembic/versions/`

### 2.3 Evaluation Engine (Phase 5 from plan)

**❌ NOT YET BUILT:**

Two-part evaluation pipeline:
1. **Atomic evaluation** (SQL):
   ```sql
   SELECT p.patient_id, ac.criterion_id, 
          (actual_age >= ac.value_numeric) as matches
   FROM patients p
   CROSS JOIN atomic_criteria ac
   WHERE ac.entity_concept_id = '21604490'  -- age concept
   ```

2. **Composite evaluation** (Recursive CTE):
   ```sql
   WITH RECURSIVE criterion_eval AS (
       -- Base: atomic results from patient_criterion_matches
       SELECT patient_id, criterion_id, matches as result
       FROM patient_criterion_matches
       JOIN atomic_criteria ac ON ...
       
       UNION ALL
       
       -- Recursive: composite evaluation
       SELECT p_id, cc.criterion_id,
              CASE cc.logic_operator
                  WHEN 'AND' THEN BOOL_AND(ce.result)
                  WHEN 'OR' THEN BOOL_OR(ce.result)
                  WHEN 'NOT' THEN NOT BOOL_OR(ce.result)
              END
       FROM criterion_eval ce
       JOIN criterion_relationships cr ON ...
       JOIN composite_criteria cc ON ...
       GROUP BY p_id, cc.criterion_id
   )
   ```

**Service/batch job needed:**
- Read atomic_criteria + patient data
- Evaluate each atomic criterion
- Use recursive CTE for composites
- Write results to patient_criterion_matches

### 2.4 Trial Eligibility Query

**❌ NOT YET BUILT:**

```sql
-- Find all eligible patients for a protocol
SELECT p.patient_id
FROM patients p
WHERE NOT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1 FROM patient_criterion_matches pcm
    JOIN atomic_criteria ac ON pcm.criterion_id = ac.id
    WHERE ac.protocol_id = ? 
      AND ac.inclusion_exclusion = 'inclusion'
      AND pcm.patient_id = p.patient_id 
      AND pcm.matches = FALSE
)
AND NOT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1 FROM patient_criterion_matches pcm
    JOIN atomic_criteria ac ON pcm.criterion_id = ac.id
    WHERE ac.protocol_id = ?
      AND ac.inclusion_exclusion = 'exclusion'
      AND pcm.patient_id = p.patient_id
      AND pcm.matches = TRUE
);
```

**API endpoint needed:**
- `GET /protocols/{protocol_id}/eligible-patients` → List patient IDs
